#6885. 6 Best Travel Dresses of 2026, Tested by a Style Writer and Travel Expert

Our top options are from Uniqlo and lululemon.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

The best travel dresses are the kind you can throw in a carry-on, forget about for hours, and still slip on looking put-together. As a style writer who’s constantly on the move (I checked off eight countries last year alone), I don’t say that lightly. I’ve learned the hard way which pieces wrinkle beyond the help of a hotel room iron, feel restrictive mid-transit, or just don’t pull their styling weight once I land. The ones worth packing work just as well with sneakers as they do with sandals, and make getting dressed in an unfamiliar place easy.

I tested 30-plus styles in real-life conditions, paying close attention to how they pack, wear, and hold up after long days of travel. The best ones strike the sweet spot between comfort and style with breathable fabrics, packable silhouettes, and just enough versatility to earn their spot in my very limited luggage space.

I’ve packed the below dresses for trips to Cancún, Paris, Marrakech, St. Lucia—and plenty of destinations in between—putting them through everything from long-haul flights to full-day itineraries. These are the ones I’ll reach for no matter where I’m headed next.

The Uniqlo Ultra Stretch AIRism Dress rings in at under $40, but the quality feels far more luxe. It’s smooth, polished, and super easy to style in a variety of ways for a single trip—meaning I can wear it multiple times without anyone clocking a repeat outfit.

The fabric is where this dress really shines, IMO. The signature AIRism material is made from 100 percent polyester, making it lightweight, breathable, and quick-drying with a soft, stretchy feel that moves with me. Whether I’m dealing with humid temps or long travel days, it keeps things cool and comfortable without clinging.

It also nails that easy, flattering fit-and-flare shape. It’s more fitted through the bust and subtly flares out toward the legs, creating a flowy drape that I especially love when walking—it just moves beautifully. The midi length hits at about mid-calf, striking that sweet spot between casual and pulled-together, so you can dress it up or down without overthinking it. I wore this dress to Barcelona during a warm September, and the breathable material and loose fit made it a major win.

And if you’re worried about packing a longer dress, don’t be. It packs down incredibly well, and any wrinkles from being in a backpack or suitcase shake out quickly before heading out the door. Plus, it has pockets! A huge win for travel days when you want to easily stash your phone, sunnies, or room key. The only downside: It’s unlined, so the white version is slightly sheer. But that also adds to its airy feel, and there are plenty of other color options if you want more coverage.

The updated lululemon Align Dress is a sporty option that can truly do it all. Designed with yoga in mind, it’s incredibly breathable, stretchy, and sweat-wicking—but doesn’t look overly athletic, thanks to its sleek, sporty-chic silhouette. It transitions seamlessly from a morning flow to a full day of sightseeing, making it one of those rare pieces I always throw into my suitcase.

The updated version improves on the original Align Dress with a nylon base and even more elastane, giving it more stretch and wrinkle resistance—yes, even after being packed down in compression cubes. Just like the brand’s famed Align leggings, this fabric feels ultra-smooth and lightweight.

Fit-wise, it features an A-line silhouette, meaning it's more fitted through the torso and gradually flares out toward the hem for a flattering drape. The mini length hits at about mid-thigh, keeping things cool and unfussy. Thoughtful built-ins like a shelf bra for light support and attached shorts with pockets make this dress perfectly practical and polished while on-the-go.

From a packing and styling perspective, it’s a no-brainer. The shorter length and featherlight fabric make it easy to toss into your suitcase and unpack with minimal wrinkles. I’ve styled it casually with a baseball cap and my fave travel shoe (shoutout to the Dr. Scholl’s Time Off Sneaker), but it also dresses up surprisingly well with a kitten heel and dainty jewelry. The only real downside is the price—it’s one of the most expensive picks on this list—but considering the quality, performance fabric, and versatility, it’s worth the investment (watch out, as it’s often on sale).

Stylish, short-sleeve dresses under $100 can be surprisingly hard to find. That’s why the Free People Blaine Midi Dress is one of my new faves. It offers just enough coverage (especially if your shoulders tend to burn in the sun like mine) while still keeping things breezy and cool. The overall vibe leans laid-back with that signature Free People look—think relaxed and boho but still put together.

Made from 100% cotton, the fabric is soft, breathable, and especially comfortable in heat and humidity. What I really love, though, is the subtly textured, crinkled finish paired with dramatic pleating, which keeps wrinkles at bay.

The fit is intentionally loose and billowy, giving it that airy, floaty shape the brand is known for. That said, its breezy fit means it runs large. My usual size worked, but I ended up using waist-cinching clips to create a bit more of a fitted shape, so I definitely recommend sizing down. It’s a midi-length dress that hits a few inches above the ankle, with thoughtful details like a stylish keyhole cutout at the back, and, yes, pockets.

This dress definitely leans casual, but in the best way. It’s great for daytime outings, exploring a new city, brunch, or even throwing over a swimsuit. I recently packed it for a trip to Mexico and it held up beautifully in the heat and humidity—easy, breezy, and styled perfectly with a simple sandal and a crochet bag.

This long-sleeve dress gives just the right amount of coverage on the arms but excels at breathability. That makes it perfect for everything from long-haul flights or bus rides to cooler evenings. It feels light and airy without sacrificing style, with an easy silhouette that still looks thoughtfully put together.

The fabric is a standout: a soft, gauzy 100% organic cotton that feels breathable and gentle against the skin. Like the Free People dress, it has a naturally textured, crinkled finish, so it doesn’t show wrinkles, even after being packed. Instead of pleating, it features a flouncy, tiered skirt with horizontal seaming, which adds movement and a bit of playful volume.

Fit-wise, it leans relaxed and slightly oversized, so sizing down is a smart move if you want a more tailored look. The mini length hits mid-thigh, creating a fun contrast with the long sleeves, while details like the subtle bell sleeves and button-down front upgrade the look. It’s one of those dresses that looks just as good styled casually as it does dressed up a bit.

It’s also incredibly versatile. The lightweight cotton makes it comfortable enough for daytime exploring while the longer sleeves give a little extra coverage when the temperature dips. It’s easy to throw on with sandals during the day or dress up with boots or jewelry at night—and just as easy to pack, thanks to that forgiving, wrinkle-resistant texture.

Meet the perfect mini dress. I own it in black, and it always looks incredibly chic—whether I’m dressing it up with heels for a city dinner or keeping it casual with sandals for daytime plans. It’s one of my favorite pieces because it feels polished no matter how you style it—coming in clutch for travel.

The fabric plays a big role in that versatility. It's made from a polyester crepe that has a subtle texture and is naturally wrinkle-resistant—ideal for tossing in a suitcase without a second thought. It holds its shape well and doesn't cling, giving it a clean, streamlined look straight out of your bag.

It has a classic, body-skimming silhouette that gently follows your shape without feeling tight or restrictive. The mini length hits at about mid-thigh, while the boat neckline adds a classy feel that instantly upgrades the overall look. It’s simple, but in that very polished, well-designed way.

It checks all the boxes for travel. The Bond Dress packs down easily, comes out looking polished, and can be styled in multiple ways depending on your itinerary. If there's a downside, it's that the streamlined fit doesn't leave much room for built-in functionality like shorts or roomy pockets, but the trade-off is a sleek, goes-with-anything mini you’ll reach for again and again.

The Dylan Mini Dress earns its spot as the most stylish pick thanks to its ultra-flattering shape and the prettiest range of colors and patterns. It has that slightly romantic feel that instantly dresses up your outfit, whether you’re heading to dinner or just wandering a new city. I always get compliments when I wear this dress (it was a major hit during my trip to Mallorca)—and it looks just as good in photos as it does in person.

The fit is where it really stands out. It has a flattering scoop neckline trimmed with delicate lace, plus a fitted bodice that dips slightly at the waist to help elongate your torso. From there, it flows into a long, airy skirt that moves beautifully as you walk. Adjustable straps let you customize the fit, and the built-in bra with removable pads adds support without the need for extra layers—always a win when packing light.

In terms of length, it hits a few inches above the ankles for that easy, breezy feel, and it comes in petite, regular, and tall options so you can find your ideal fit. The longer length gives it a slightly dressier edge, while still feeling relaxed enough for daytime wear. Despite the longer length, I don’t have any issues packing this dress in my suitcase when I pop it into a packing cube.

Style-wise, it leans pretty and girly in the best way. It’s perfect for summer travel or warm-weather destinations, but also transitions nicely into cooler conditions with a simple cardigan layered on top. The only con? Popular colors and patterns tend to sell out fast—so if you see a style available in your size, I recommend snagging it ASAP.

Since traveling involves lots of packing, travel expert Addie Bell says to look for wrinkle-resistant materials like spandex, nylon, or polyester. These fabrics are great for maintaining a polished look after being packed in a suitcase. Travel days often include a lot of walking, so you'll also want to make sure your dress's fabric is breathable—cotton and linen are great choices, but they are prone to wrinkling.

If you’re only traveling with a carry-on or trying to maximize storage, stick to neutral tones like black, navy, white, and beige, which are versatile and easy to mix and match with other wardrobe pieces. "Subtle patterns can also help hide wrinkles and stains, making the dress more travel-friendly," says Bell. But bright colors can add a fun, vibrant touch. "I always recommend sticking within a similar color palette to ensure they match other items you’re bringing.”

As a Women’s Health style writer, I travel during my PTO and I travel for work while scoping out destinations for WH’s Travel Awards. For the past few months, I’ve researched and tested the best travel dresses for jet-setting in style and comfort. In addition to testing dresses based on durability, material, style, and their ability to stand up to lots of movement and long hours, I also consulted travel expert Addie Bell, founder and CEO of Jetset and Travel, a travel consulting agency.

#6881. 8 Best Travel Pants for Women, Tested by Travel Experts and Editors

You won't be counting down the minutes to take these off.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

My passport book is nearly out of blank pages, and I’m starting to worry that my next trip will require an emergency upgrade to make it through customs. But as a frequent flyer and style writer, one thing I don’t stress over is what to wear to the airport. Between maxing out my PTO and logging plenty of miles for work (including tons of in-transit testing for WH Travel Awards), I’ve mastered the art of the airport fit. And while I firmly believe that wearing jeans on a plane should land you on the no-fly list, I’m all about wearing something comfortable without looking like I’m wearing pajamas at the terminal.

That’s where the right pair of travel pants comes in clutch, hitting that happy medium between polished and comfortable. I’m talking about pants you can sit in for hours, sprint through a terminal in, and still wear straight to dinner without a second thought. Over time (and many, many flights), I’ve narrowed down the pairs that truly deliver: versatile, low-maintenance styles that work just as well in your suitcase as they do on your body, from takeoff to touchdown.

The Vuori Halo Essential Wideleg pant has been at the top of my drawer since I got my first pair nearly a year ago. I’m obsessed with the super-soft and smooth polyester and elastane blend. It feels nearly pajama-like while looking way more polished than your average sweatpant (especially if you opt for a darker color like black or navy).

I often reach for this pant when getting dressed for the airport because I know I’ll be comfortable on the plane and that I’ll wear it at least once during my trip. The wide-leg silhouette skims the body in a really flattering way without looking baggy or oversized. It looks great paired with Vuori’s matching zip-up and my fave Dr. Scholl’s Time Off Sneaker for long-haul flights, but it just as easily dresses up with a cute sandal and a form-fitting tank or tee.

Oh, and the pant never wrinkles. It feels soft and cotton-like but since it's made from a buttery synthetic blend, it always looks sleek—more quiet luxury than airport chaos. The material isn’t too thick either, so I don’t find myself overheating while wearing it, even when I land in a sunny destination. I wore this pant while traveling to Mexico recently and felt totally comfortable from takeoff in New York until I arrived at the hotel.

If you’re looking for an under-$50 find that holds its own against pricier pairs, the Old Navy Linen-Blend Ankle Pant is it. Made from a 55% linen blend, it delivers the airy look and feel you want from linen—without the steep price tag most comparable styles carry.

Comfort-wise, it’s exactly what I want for a travel day: lightweight, breathable, and stylish. The secret is in the fabric. This pant blends linen with rayon, a semi-synthetic fiber derived from natural sources like wood pulp that’s known for being soft, smooth, and less prone to creasing than 100% linen. The result is breezy but cozy fabric that doesn’t feel remotely scratchy or itchy. I also love the clean, straight-leg silhouette and high waist that makes me feel put-together with next to zero effort.

I will say, this is one of those pairs I prefer to wear on the plane rather than pack, since it picks up a few more wrinkles when folded in a suitcase (it is still linen, after all). But once I put it on, the fabric bounces back nicely—and I end up reaching for it constantly throughout my trip. The pant is endlessly versatile: I’ll throw it on with flat sandals and a white tee for daytime exploring, then swap in a blouse and heels for dinner without missing a beat.

If you didn’t know better, you might mistake the lululemon Daydrift High-Rise Wide-Leg Trouser for a lounge pant—but one mirror check and it’s giving Succession-coded energy. It’s my go-to for when I need to land and immediately be “on,” whether that’s heading to a meeting or traveling with colleagues.

Made primarily from nylon and elastane, this pant is breathable, sweat-wicking, and cool to the touch—exactly what I want for a long day on the go. It has a smooth, tailored look that resists wrinkling, making it especially suitcase-friendly. It also features a stretch waistband with an internal drawstring, which makes a big difference when I’m sitting for hours or dealing with the general unpredictability of travel days.

I’ll wear it with my go-to Uniqlo Crew Neck T-Shirt and sneakers for a more casual, on-the-move look, but it just as easily leans corporate with a button-down or blazer. Swap in a silky top and heels and it even holds its own for evening plans. It’s that comfort and versatility that make it such a staple in my everyday and travel wardrobe.

If you want something that feels barely there for a warm-weather vacation, the FP Movement Champ Is Here Pant is my top rec. Made from 100% nylon, it’s airy and flowy and allows for total free range of movement—perfect for long travel days or more active itineraries. And thanks to the barrel-leg silhouette (meaning it’s a bit wider at the knee area) and the subtle contrast piping, it still feels on-trend.

Its nylon construction is naturally lightweight, quick-drying, and impressively durable, which is great if your trip involves anything beyond just walking through airports and lounging around. The water-resistant finish and a reinforced woven design help resist tearing and ripping, so it holds up whether you’re caught in a drizzle, heading out on a hike, or just putting it through its paces. But it still feels soft and breathable—not stiff or overly technical.

“I’m a big fan of track pants, and the second I bought this pair, I had travel in mind,” says associate editor Olivia Luppino. “The open, barrel-leg fit makes it super comfortable and yet fashionable to wear on a plane—and I’ve also worn it on a hike, as a beach cover-up, and even dressed it up for dinner.” I’ve found the same—it looks great with sneakers and a tank for daytime, or a fitted top and sandals for nighttime. The only small caveat is the rise: it does sit quite high, which can feel like a lot if you have a shorter torso (I sometimes fold the waistband down for a more comfortable fit).

If you’ve ever tried to juggle your phone, passport, lip balm, and boarding pass while sprinting through a terminal, you get the value of a pocketed pair of pants. The Athleta Skyline High Rise Barrel Leg Pant is my fave in the category. The deep, secure pockets make a huge difference when I’m on the go and don’t want to constantly dig through my bag like I’m Mary Poppins.

Made from polyester, cotton, and spandex, it has a structured, almost denim-like feel—but with way more stretch. The pant has a polished, slightly tailored look, but with the kind of softness and flexibility that makes long flights and all-day wear far more comfortable. The breathable fabric allows for airflow and quick-drying properties that come in handy if you’re on the move (or caught in less-than-ideal weather). There’s even UPF 40+ protection for extra coverage on sunny days. I sort of think of it as an upgraded cargo pant— functional, but not in a “I just came from REI” kind of way.

The barrel-leg silhouette adds a slightly relaxed shape that still feels put-together, and the material isn’t too bulky. I love that this pant works for air travel, city exploring, light hikes, and casual dinners. The only downside? The slightly more structured fabric means they’re not quite as soft or drapey as some of the other pairs on this list—but I still find the fit super flattering.

I usually don’t wear sweatpants during travel because they read way too casual compared to a jogger or trousers. But for longer flights, I sometimes make an exception—and this is the pair I’ll reach for. The straight-leg silhouette feels a bit more put-together than most other sweatpants, but it’s still super soft and lightweight. This sweatpant looks great with almost any casual fit, but our style team loves wearing it with Alo’s color-coordinating jackets and hoodies for a chic, monochrome vibe.

It’s made with a French terry cloth (a cotton blend that’s smooth on the outside) and stylist Alexandra Juric says this makes for a soft, breathable fleece lining. It has a slightly oversized look, but if you want a more snug fit, you can size down. However, I’m into the oversized look and love that this pair doesn’t fit too tightly on the lower belly, since other popular sweatpants I’ve tested can emphasize that area too much. Style contributor Lily Wohlner also prefers it a bit bigger, as she can always roll down the waistband for a more snug fit around the hips.

“The only downside to these sweats is the length; the pants go well past my ankle, making them hard to wear outside,” says Juric. But, sizing down, rolling the waistband, or wearing a platform sneaker would likely solve that problem.

The Vince Bias Cut High Waist Pant is easily the most stylish pair of travel pants in my rotation. It has a liquid-like drape that instantly makes any outfit feel more luxe and the fit is incredibly flattering. I wore it nonstop on my last trip to Spain—on the plane, out to dinner, even just wandering during the day—and it somehow always looked chic with minimal effort.

It’s made from a blend of acetate (a semi-synthetic fiber derived from wood pulp—known for a silky feel and subtle sheen) and polyester, which gives this pant that drapey fit. The bias cut, which refers to cutting the fabric diagonally across the grain, allows the material to skim the body rather than cling to it, creating a fluid effect that I find super flattering. It also gives the pant a natural stretch and tons of movement, so it doesn’t feel restrictive. Add in the high waist and the range of chic neutrals it comes in and you have a truly classic-looking pant.

During my last trip, I styled this pant with a simple tank and sandals during the day, then swapped in a blouse and heels at night. It packs relatively well, too, thanks to that fluid fabric—and because it’s so lightweight, it doesn’t take up much space in a carry-on. The only downside is the care: it’s hand wash, line dry, which isn’t always the most convenient if you need to freshen up mid-trip.

If comfort is your top priority, the Skims Cotton Jersey Foldover Pant is the softest pair in the lineup. This is the kind of pant I put on for a flight and immediately feel better—like I’m wearing my favorite pajamas but still look pulled together enough to step off the plane without a full outfit change. It’s an ideal pant for long-haul days, early morning departures, or anytime you just want to feel cozy without fully leaning into sleepwear or baggy silhouettes.

It’s made from a jersey blend (mostly cotton with a touch of elastane), which is what gives it that ultra-soft, slightly stretchy feel. Cotton keeps things breathable and gentle against the skin, while elastane adds just enough give for when you’re sitting, walking, or curled up in a window seat. The foldover waistband is a standout feature—it lets you customize the rise depending on your preference, whether you want a more low-slung, relaxed fit or a bit more coverage. The fabric has that smooth, second-skin quality that moves with you, making it especially great for travel days when comfort is non-negotiable.

That said, it’s more versatile than you might expect. I’ll wear it with a simple tank and sneakers for an easy airport look, but it also works with a structured jacket to feel a bit more put-together. It also packs down to almost nothing, making it a great backup option in your carry-on. The one trade-off is that it does lean more casual—so while it’s perfect for the plane and low-key plans, it’s not quite as polished as some of the other options here.

Packing smarter starts with choosing capsule wardrobe essentials that pull double (or triple) duty—think a crisp button-down that you can wear over a tank or tucked into trousers, a lightweight knit that works on the plane and at dinner, or a simple slip dress that doubles as a beach coverup and dinner outfit. Travel pants should follow that same philosophy: versatile enough to style casually with sneakers and a tee, but polished enough to pair with a blouse or blazer. Building multiple outfits around one reliable piece avoids overpacking and makes dressing while on vacation way easier.

Since studying abroad in 2019, I’ve been traveling nonstop. I’ve visited nearly 30 countries so far (8 last year alone!), and learned exactly which travel clothes are best for packing and wearing on long-haul flights and train rides. Plus, as a style writer for Women’s Health, I also travel for work while scoping out destinations for WH’s Travel Awards. For the past few months, I’ve researched and tested the best travel pants for jet-setting in style and comfort alongside style contributor Lily Wohlner and associate editor Olivia Luppino. In addition to testing pants based on comfort, durability, versatility, and style, I also consulted a travel expert for her guidance.

#6878. The 10 Best Sports Bras, Tested by Fitness and Style Editors

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

Finding the right sports bra is crucial. That’s because a well-fitted one keeps your girls locked in and comfortable during jogs, stretches, and squats, so you don’t have to worry about spillage or bounce.

To find the best of the best sports bras, our fitness and style editors tested more than 50 options—from high-impact sports bras designed to minimize bounce to softer options from our fave activewear brands that prioritize comfort. We evaluated each pick for support, fit, comfort, and performance across a range of workouts and body types.

Read more: Alo Super Sleek Bra Tank Review

This bra is a 2026 Fitness Awards winner, and basically “the holy grail of sports bras for high-intensity workouts,” says executive health and fitness director Jacqueline Andriakos. It’s ultra-comfortable with adjustable straps for a customizable fit that works for all sorts of exercises.

The Performance Flex Bra offers medium support, but we’ve found it minimizes bounce without restricting movement, thanks to a higher neckline and light built-in padding that helps me feel secure while I train. In testing, it felt just as comfortable during slower sessions like yoga and Pilates as it did during higher-intensity workout classes. The hold is supportive without feeling overly compressive, so the girls stay in place without that squeezed-in feeling some medium-support bras can have.

One of our favorite details is the padding. “It’s built in—so you never have to deal with shifting, scrunching, or fishing pads out of the laundry,” Andriakos says. That means the bra keeps its shape wash after wash and eliminates the annoyance of removable inserts. The padding also stays comfortable through body changes—Andriakos says it even supported her during pregnancy and postpartum. The only potential downside is that the padding isn’t removable, which might not appeal to those who want less coverage.

The wide adjustable straps are another favorite feature of mine. They distribute weight evenly across my shoulders without digging in or creating pressure points during longer workouts. Even better, the straps adjust from the front, so it’s way easier to adjust than traditional bras.

And for my sweaty gals, the fabric is perfect for keeping perspiration under control. It’s made from a breathable, sweat-wicking blend of recycled polyester and elastane, which feels soft and flexible while maintaining shape. It keeps me cool and comfortable—even in heated classes—and the full mesh racerback panel adds tons of ventilation. That open, breathable design helps release heat during workouts. It also gives the bra a streamlined silhouette that sits smoothly under all my workout tops.

This bra is a favorite among four (!) WH editors—myself, Andriakos, Wohlner, and Lauture. We love its support during high-impact workouts, durability, value, and stay-in-place cups that move like a second skin.

It has a slight compressed feel, which keeps everything secure and minimizes bounce during plyometric moves and running, but it’s not so tight that it restricts movement. The first time I pulled on the Forme Power+ Bra, I instinctively rolled my shoulders forward like I usually do at my desk—except this time, they didn’t stay there. Within seconds, the bra gently guided them back, opening up my chest and lifting my upper spine so I stood noticeably taller without consciously trying. It’s a subtle shift, but one you feel immediately: less hunching, more length through your torso, and a sense that your upper body is quietly being coached into better alignment.

The Power+ is made from a compressive blend of polyester, nylon, and spandex and features multiple tension fabrics with strategically placed, double-layered support panels throughout the back and shoulders. Together, they create a gentle pull that encourages your shoulders to stay back and your chest open. The result feels supportive without restricting movement, and I love wearing it for all my workouts.

Despite its posture-focused design, the Power+ also functions as a solid sports bra. The compression keeps everything comfortably locked in during strength sessions, cardio workouts, and high-impact movement, while the breathable fabric prevents that heavy, trapped-in-sweat feeling some compressive bras create. Wide straps, generous arm openings, and a full-coverage back also help smooth things out and give the bra a flattering, secure fit.

Another unexpected perk: It’s just as helpful during everyday wear as it is in the gym. Whether you’re sitting at a laptop for hours or driving to a workout class, the bra keeps your shoulders from rounding forward and helps reduce some of that end-of-day neck and upper-back tension I know too well.

When looking for a sports bra, you should know the difference between compression and encapsulation in your bra. Compression, which can be found in the Nike or Lululemon sports bras, offers the feeling of your boobs being so supported that they’re a bit squished and pushed down. Encapsulation lifts, cradles, and separates your girls while still eliminating boob bounce. It can be found in the Beyond Yoga bra. #6874. Cisco Intros Privileged Infrastructure Access for People and AI Agents

Cisco has introduced Privileged Infrastructure Access, an approach to infrastructure administration that gives people and AI agents temporary, task-specific permissions instead of leaving standing privileges available after their work is done.

In a recent blog post, Cisco's Matt Caulfield described a system that uses cryptographic identity to connect an administrator or agent to the infrastructure needed for a particular task. Access is governed by short-lived privileges, with session recording providing a record of the work performed.

"If there is no work being done, no privilege exists," Caulfield wrote. Cisco positions the offering as a way to bring identity controls directly into infrastructure operations while reducing dependence on static credentials, shared secrets, and broad access.

The announcement follows Cisco's previously announced Teleport partnership and its Infrastructure Identity concept, which connects the identity of a person or machine with the resources it can access, the actions it performs, and the permissions governing those actions.

Caulfield described a gap between the controls organizations apply to workforce applications and those used to administer network infrastructure. Companies have invested in single sign-on, multifactor authentication, and risk-based controls for application access, he wrote, but those protections often do not follow an engineer logging into a router or switch.

Instead, infrastructure access may depend on long-lived credentials, shared accounts, or separate policy systems. According to Cisco, that separation makes it harder to enforce least privilege and establish which person or system performed a particular action.

The company illustrates the problem with an administrator updating hundreds of switches during a maintenance window. If some updates fail, the administrator needs to troubleshoot before the window closes. The permissions available to handle that work, however, may cover more infrastructure and remain active longer than the task requires.

Traditional privileged access management can reduce risk through credential vaulting and rotation, Caulfield wrote. But he argued that operational friction can undermine those controls when teams create exceptions to complete urgent work. Those exceptions can leave credentials and privileges usable after the immediate need has passed.

Cisco's stated objective is to give infrastructure teams a consistent route to the devices they manage while providing security teams with policy enforcement, temporary permissions, and a record of activity. The company describes operational convenience as part of the design, rather than treating access governance solely as a credential-management problem.

Privileged Infrastructure Access makes access an on-demand action, according to the announcement. Cryptographic identity establishes the actor, and the resulting connection provides only the privileges needed to complete the task. Those privileges expire when the task concludes.

For customers using Cisco Identity Services Engine (ISE) and Terminal Access Controller Access-Control System Plus (TACACS+), the offering can draw on existing policy and device context to govern access to Cisco routers and switches. Cisco says customers can extend the same access model to other infrastructure, including servers, Kubernetes clusters, and cloud resources.

OpenAI has announced it will retire Custom GPTs and move the specialized workflows built around them to a new plugin architecture spanning ChatGPT and its Codex coding agent.

Enterprise AI spending is growing faster than the number of organizations paying for it, according to a new Menlo Ventures report that points to a shift from buying access for employees to financing work performed by agents.

According to a survey by data and AI services provider Further, enterprises are reporting benefits from artificial intelligence even as their data preparation and oversight processes remain incomplete.

Today's middle school students' future careers will be transformed by artificial intelligence, automation, robotics, advanced manufacturing, biotechnology, cybersecurity, and technologies that may not yet exist. Our career education policies need to prepare them for that reality.

#6872. The 10 Most Comfortable Bras, According to Style Editors

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

I’ve tested more bras than I can count as a style writer. And over the years, I’ve learned that comfort is a nonnegotiable—if it digs, pinches, or rides up, it doesn’t deserve a space in my drawer. The thing is, no two boobs are exactly alike (not even your own). One might sit higher, be fuller, or have a different shape than the other. So finding the right bra isn’t about hitting a standard size—it’s about understanding your body. According to Navya Mysore, MD, a primary care physician and Women’s Health advisor, the right bra boosts comfort, improves posture, and eases back pain by evenly distributing breast tissue. If your band rides up, your straps slide, or your underwire doesn’t lie flat, it’s a clear sign something’s off.

And if your usual size suddenly stops working? Totally normal. Bra fitter and undergarment educator Kimmay Caldwell says weight changes, hormone shifts, or your monthly cycle can impact your size. To make it trickier, some brands still use outdated sizing methods (like the “plus four” rule), while others go by your exact underbust. Bottom line: your size can change and vary between clothing brands.

All of that can make bra shopping feel like a bit of a minefield, which is exactly why I tested 20 different styles across fits, fabrics, and support levels to find the ones that are actually worth your money. These ten passed the wear test and then some, proving that comfort, support, and style actually can exist in one bra.

A bra should fit your body—not the other way around—and Knix totally got the memo with this one. It feels snug at first, but it softens and molds to your shape with every wear. What really impressed me was the fit because it almost feels custom, thanks to the ultra-thin foam that contours without adding volume.

Plus, the sleek fabric (a nylon-spandex blend) moves with you, feeling super smooth against the skin and breathable enough to wear for hours.

If you're after definition without the discomfort, this wire-free option def delivers. The molded cups lift and separate, while subtle side padding helps prevent spillage and sideboob. The result? A natural shape, no extra bulk, and no awkward lines under tighter tops. And with 99 sizes (!!) and eight colors, the options are actually inclusive.

The only downside? It’s not the best for high movement and workouts. I find myself adjusting it when I bend or move quickly. Still, for everyday comfort and a super flattering fit, this one’s a standout.

If you love the shape and support of a traditional bra but can't stand the feel of underwire, the Shapermint Essentials Sweetheart Wireless Contour Bra is your happy medium. It delivers a surprisingly lifted, rounded silhouette without a single wire digging into your ribs, making it my top pick for anyone who wants all-day comfort.

The lightly contoured foam cups provide just enough structure to prevent the dreaded uniboob, while the wide, stretchy band stays comfortably in place without pinching or rolling. The smooth, buttery fabric feels soft against the skin and disappears nicely under T-shirts and other fitted tops.

I was especially impressed by how supportive it feels for a wireless bra. It lifts and shapes without feeling restrictive, and the wider straps help distribute weight so your shoulders don't pay the price after hours of wear. It's one of those bras you forget you're wearing—which is pretty much the highest compliment I can give.

Push-up bras with underwires have a bad rep, and for good reason. They’re usually stiff, pokey, and downright uncomfortable. But after wearing this Maidenform bra for months (yes, months), I can confidently say it’s a major exception to the rule.

From the start, this bra felt different. The underwire doesn’t dig or jab, thanks to its soft, stretchy crochet lace that moves with you. The cups are unpadded but still offer that ideal medium support and lift, enough to give shape and a gentle push-up effect, but never so much that I feel squeezed or overly sculpted. It’s supportive without being suffocating, which is rare in the world of push-ups.And let’s talk about the straps. They’re fully adjustable and convertible, so whether I’m going for a classic or criss-cross fit, I can tweak the style to match my outfit. That kind of versatility makes it feel like you’re getting two bras in one (and who doesn’t love a multitasker?).The lift it gives is subtle but effective. It’s not the dramatic “hello, cleavage” kind of push-up, but more of a natural, perky boost that works under everything from tees to low-cut dresses. No spillage, just a flattering shape that makes me feel more confident in whatever I’m wearing. Bonus points for the range of styles: It comes in smooth fabric options to wear under clingy tees, and lace versions for when you want to have a little more fun.One of my biggest bra fears? The dreaded back and side bulge. But this bra’s smoothing side panels genuinely help—there’s no weird digging or over-compression, just a smooth, flattering fit that doesn’t fight with your body. Long story short: I tested it, I lived in it, and I’m keeping it in my regular rotation.

As someone who hates wearing bras (the wires, the bulk, the digging), this is the one I wear most. Made for fuller busts (DD+), the Cosabella Evolution Curvy Bralette lifts and shapes with lightly molded cups, no wires necessary.

“It’s the only bralette I’ve found that can actually support my chest without feeling restrictive,” says style contributor Lily Wohlner. The soft, stretchy fabric, made from a smooth microfiber that consists of 85 percent polyamide and 15 percent elastane, molds to your body, starting snug and quickly adapting for a custom-like fit. “I will say, mine have stretched a bit over the past few years, so it might be time to re-up,” she says.

This bra provides exceptional support for larger busts, even without a heavy-duty wire band. Instead, it makes use of an elasticized band that provides a surprisingly large amount of support without digging into the skin. It also has extra support at the sides to prevent spillage and to keep everything looking seamless, even under thin t-shirts and tight dresses.

The adjustable straps and underband stay put, and the side coverage smooths everything out—no spillage, no weird lines under shirts. It gives a natural, rounded shape that works under pretty much anything.

If you tend to scoop the girls into place to make them look perkier (a good trick!), this Cuup bra does the work for you. The rounded neckline and unlined, unpadded cups naturally lift and shape, while seamlessly extending into the straps for clean lines and a flattering fit.

It runs snug, but that’s what makes it so supportive—my boobs stay put and look perkier without any adjusting. The underwire is super lightweight and flexible, so there’s zero digging or poking there. I also love that the cups are unlined, because it makes the bra feel super lightweight and barely there.

The bra is made from a microfiber blend of polyamide and elastane, so it’s buttery-smooth, stretches with you while keeping its shape, and is ultra-breathable. It’s great for warm days since it feels like next to nothing on the skin and helps wick moisture away. The microfiber is also great for pairing with thin or tight tops, since it's basically undetectable under clothing—no lines in sight.

The straps are fully adjustable for a customizable lift, and even though the cups are thin, they still offer enough coverage to prevent any chilly-day surprises, IYKYK. Since the cups run a bit small, sizing up might be your best bet for full coverage.

The Honeylove CrossOver Bra is ultra-comfortable, smoothing, and supportive. The nylon and spandex blend feels soft and wearable for hours without digging into your skin while providing a completely seamless silhouette from every angle. I always wear this pick under thinner tops, tight dresses, and clingy sweaters because it creates a totally streamlined look under clothes while lifting the girls and supporting the underarm area and sides.

Plus, the bonded cradle under the bust mimics the structure of an underwire, delivering firm support without any poking or pinching. The full mesh overlay adds light compression and lift, adding to the sleek look.

This bra also has one of the widest size ranges on our list, which is a major win. However, it does run a little big, so if you're between sizes, consider sizing down for the best fit.

Finding a lace bra that's actually comfortable—especially if you have a larger bust—can feel like a tall order, but the Bravissimo Millie Bra proves you don't have to sacrifice support for something pretty. The most comfortable bras usually come down to the right blend of fabrics. Here’s what each one brings to the table:

Comfort is about more than just size—it’s also about ensuring the wire doesn’t create pressure points or cause discomfort throughout the day. Look for bras with flexible, padded, or cushioned underwires, which offer support without digging into your skin. The underwire should sit flat against your ribcage and follow the natural curve of your breasts. If it feels like it’s poking or shifting, it’s not the right fit. If you prefer a wire-free bra, opt for styles with flexible boning, molded cups, and wide, flexible bands to provide support.

The most accurate way to find your size is to get fitted in person. Once you have your size, your new bra should feel snug and secure. The band should be comfortably tight, but not dig into the skin. A good rule of thumb is that you should be able to fit two fingers between the band and your skin, but no more. The cups should also lie flat against your chest.

The Women’s Health team has been testing bras since we first wrote this story in 2023. To find the most comfortable options, our team wore each style for at least a few weeks at a time. We wore them under different circumstances, including workouts, lounging, and long days at the office. But our main criterion for each bra was comfort. I also spoke with two bra experts and personally tested 20-plus bras over several years to lock down the ones I actually wear on repeat.

I recently re-tested a bunch of options and tapped style contributor Lily Wohlner for an update to this story to boost our list and keep it up-to-date. The final guide includes everything from bralettes and seamless bras to push-up bras—and I actually wear all of them in my day-to-day life.

#6869. The Story Behind Peggy Scott’s Wedding Dress

The Gilded Age threw the wedding of the year for the fan-favorite character, played by Denée Benton

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

It’s 88 degrees on a humid June day in Troy, New York, and Denée Benton stands in a local church’s outbuilding, posing in Peggy Scott’s wedding dress, not breaking a sweat.

It has been a long, hot day of filming, and the cast and crew are scheduled to keep going for many more hours. To get to this photo shoot, I had to walk past a cramped church hallway where Cynthia Nixon, Audra McDonald, and John Douglas Thompson sat together in various stages of Gilded Age undress, trading inside jokes. Everyone on set is in a state of elation, both because of the long-planned-for episode featuring the wedding of Peggy and Dr. William Kirkland and because it’s the day of Game 4 of the Knicks’s historic NBA run. A few hours after I leave set, Nixon will post a reel of the cast reacting to the Knicks win in their Gilded Age finery. But even amidst all of this excitement, Benton in her wedding dress steals the show.

Writers, production assistants, costume designers, and even Jordan Donica, who plays Dr. Kirkland, gather to watch the shoot. Benton, in the middle of it all, is poised in the gown of white lace, a corset underneath, dress lines crisp, makeup unsmudged, a picture of bridal serenity. Later, Kasia Walicka Maimone, the show’s costume designer, will tell me she spied Benton doing breathing exercises in the church pews, just to maintain her stamina.

Peggy has been a fan favorite since her introduction in the show’s first episode. An ambitious, upper-class Black woman in Gilded Age America, she is an anomaly in period television. Her storyline has introduced viewers to the world of the Black elite, a burgeoning class that emerged during Reconstruction and would dominate Black political and cultural life to the present day. Popular nonfiction books like Our Kind of People have documented the history of this group, but they have been largely absent from television. Even Black weddings, of any time period, are rarely portrayed on American TV. So how do you design a dress for a wedding that hasn’t been seen before?

“The Gilded Age,” as the period between the 1870s and 1890s is known, “was famous for its excitement [around fashion],” says Walicka Maimone. “How do we represent this excitement? How do we pull out from the historical research things that we, the modern audience, are going to be excited about? We have this giant library of about 50 to 60,000 images that we’ve collected since season 1 … but there’s always manipulation that I apply to it.” Peggy’s wedding dress “resonates with a lot of elements from the period, but it’s not a direct quote of any one specific dress.…What was really important to both Denée and me was the composite of the silhouette [and] the texture of the lace,” a detail that was “referred to in the script directly by her mother-in-law.”

“The speed that we had to accomplish the design and execution and put it on the screen was astronomical,” Walicka Maimone says. “Hundreds of hands altogether contribute to the making. Each dress takes about six to eight weeks to be built.” Those working on the final bridal look included the in-house costume-design team, milliners for the veil and hat, a jeweler in Rome who designed Peggy’s earrings, and on-set tailors who maintained the costume during filming.

“My wedding dress had over 75 buttons going down the back. Amber, my head dresser, buttoned each one by hand. The craftsmanship was really unbelievable,” Benton tells me a few months later. “It felt powerful to me, spiritually. It felt like we were embodying a moment for a lot of people. I have never seen a luxury Black wedding of the 1800s on any screen, on a stage, in any popular media. And so it really feels like we’re getting to be a part of a pop-culture history moment.”

Peggy is a child of post-Reconstruction America. Her father, Arthur, experienced enslavement, but she is part of the first generation of Black people born into freedom and citizenship. The thrill of watching her on screen is seeing a character who relishes pushing against the boundaries of what that might mean. She is both an avatar of modern viewers’ desires and a fantasy of what Black womanhood might have looked like in a time of dynamic social change and fabulous dressing. For Peggy, the question of a wedding and marriage takes on additional significance.

“Because the law of the land during enslavement recognized enslaved people as property, [and] therefore not protected by the law, nor were there unions, whether they were chosen unions or forced unions … they could be separated at any moment. The law did not protect the sanctity of marriage for Black people, for enslaved people,” explains historian Erica Armstrong Dunbar, who is chair of the African-American Studies department at Emory University, in addition to a consultant on The Gilded Age.

Marriage for the Gilded Age generation of Black people was an assertion of agency and an act of claiming Black humanity. The ability to choose partners and form Black families—and have those family bonds legally protected—was a radical act in the aftermath of slavery. All of this history can inform the context of Peggy Scott’s marriage, but what that wedding might look like on screen is another question.

“When we first started talking about the season and the wedding, Sonja Warfield [the episode’s writer] and Julian Fellowes [The Gilded Age’s creator] were very invested in the notion of the jumping of the broom and that being a part of African American wedding tradition,” Armstrong Dunbar explains. Jumping the broom was a wedding tradition practiced by some enslaved people, a ceremonial act to mark a bond that by definition was not legally recognized.

“I immediately was like, but that’s not really happening in the 1880s,” Armstrong Dunbar explains. “People were actually distancing themselves from many of the traditional experiences connected to the weddings of enslaved people.”

Fellowes, Warfield, and Gilded Age executive producer Salli Richardson-Whitfield and producer Davita Scarlett, however, were all adamant about including this practice in the wedding episode. Many of them had participated in broom-jumping ceremonies in their own weddings; the practice became popular again in the 1970s, ’80s, and ’90s as post–Civil Rights era couples looked to include specifically African American rituals in their wedding ceremonies.

But part of the pleasure of The Gilded Age is watching how the show plays with historical conventions and anachronisms to further draw viewers into characters’ lives. In this case, the writers and producers decided to give Peggy’s father, Arthur, who was once enslaved, the task of handing the broom he used in his wedding ceremony to his daughter, a moment that recognizes the family’s beginnings and its triumph of seeing a freeborn daughter legally wed. “It really encapsulated the way in which people try to meld traditions when they’re bringing their families together,” says Scarlett. “It just felt too delicious and too interesting and too thematically rich to pass up.”

“I knew that we were going to be doing Peggy getting married, and I claimed it. I said, ‘No one else is allowed to direct this wedding but me,’ ” says director Richardson-Whitfield. “I just wanted to make sure that it was as lush and gorgeous and special as anything else that we’ve done on the show.”

This included the decision to feature a gospel choir. “We specifically chose also to do it a cappella and not have any instruments with the music because I felt like those beautiful Black voices were enough to fill the church,” she says. An a cappella chorus singing spirituals would have been, like the broom jumping, frowned upon by many upper-class Black people in the 1880s, but that tension provided another opportunity to deepen the wedding scenes. “Just because we are African American people doesn’t mean we all think the same thing,” Richardson-Whitfield says. “It’s nice to be able to explore different kinds of Black people.”

The show’s commitment to nuance and its willingness to acknowledge the historical existence of different kinds of marginalized people means its fan base stretches far and wide. Recently, the creative agency Little Big World hosted a Gilded Age drag ball and panel on queer black history with Benton, activist and Gilded Age fan Brittany Packnett Cunningham, and queer historian Hugh Ryan. The event was packed.

“I think that people see Peggy as this sort of doll princess,” Benton tells me, “but as the actor playing her … bell hooks has this quote that’s like, ‘Queer not as in who I sleep with, but queer as in at odds with everything around me.’ That is Peggy. Period. There was not really a class of professional women [during the Gilded Age]; you were either aristocracy or you were working class. And then to be a Black professional, she really is one of one moving through this space in her time. And I find that to be very queer.”

“That’s one of the two powers of history work for me,” says Ryan. “One is seeing … ‘Oh, things actually were really different back then. So that means they could be really different in the future.’ And I think that's liberatory. But the other part, and this is where I think The Gilded Age shines … is getting people excited. I was in a panel with 60 Black queer folk in a Black queer space in Bed-Stuy talking about the Gilded Age and Black drag kings in vaudeville. That conversation only happens because beautiful art sparks our interest and allows us to see a place in the past.”

“As an artist, you can’t really ask for more than to do something that is popular and meaningful at the same time,” Benton says. “The sweet spot.”

#6868. The 10 Best Workout Tops for Women of 2026, Tested by Experts and Editors

Our fave option on this list is from Beyond Yoga.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

I’ve spent years logging miles, reps, and classes in all types of activewear; which means I’ve tested more workout tops than I can count. From Pilates to outdoor runs to heavy lifting sessions, I know exactly what separates a top you forget you’re wearing from one you can’t wait to peel off. For me, the best workout tops for women nail comfort, offer just enough support, and look flattering.

The last thing you want is an overly tight top that clips your movement (especially during yoga!), but it should still provide enough compression to hold you and the girls in, says Rebecca Stewart, CPT, a trainer and mobility coach. While you could make do with a regular cotton t-shirt during your workout sesh, it won't offer the same level of support, mobility, or sweat-wicking capabilities.

Finding the right workout top all comes down to personal preference. Because there are so many options to choose from—think T-shirts, tanks, and long-sleeve tops—we asked experts and tested tons of options ourselves to find styles we think will fit a wide range of needs and preferences. From lightweight, flowy options to compression shirts great for high-intensity workouts, our absolute favorite workout tops below are highlighted below.

Over the last few months I’ve tested a handful of workout tops and the Beyond Yoga Spacedye Slim Racerback Cropped Tank topped my list for a few reasons: It’s super comfortable (it won a spot for the softest bra in our Best Racerback Bras story!), has a built-in shelf bra, is size inclusive, and comes in a ton of vibrant and neutral shades.

It's basically the two-in-one bra and workout tank combo you didn’t know you needed. Made from the brand’s signature polyester and elastane blend, it’s the comfiest and most lightweight option I’ve ever worn. It feels like you're wearing nothing at all, but it still keeps the girls in place with a medium-level compression that’s perfect for light to moderate workouts. The cherry on top? It’s moisture-wicking, so it keeps you dry whether you’re working out or just running around on a hot day.

Our only gripe: If you have a bigger chest, you’ll probably want to layer a sports bra underneath for extra support. But if you're part of the itty bitty titty committee, you're good to go wearing this solo pretty much anywhere.

ICYMI, Amazon Essentials Activewear is where it’s at for affordable workout basics that rival the quality of some of the more luxe brands we’ve tried. The cotton, modal, and elastane blend feels thick, substantial, and smooth to the touch. It’s the kind of material that holds up well throughout repeated workouts and wash cycles, so you can keep this workout tee in constant rotation.

We also love the subtle crop length that isn’t too short or too long. It gives you room to move without showing too much skin, and has a semi-relaxed fit that perfectly skims the body. It’s extremely soft, but thanks to the touch of elastane in the fabric, it has ample stretch so you don’t feel constricted during overhead lifts or higher-impact workouts like running or HIIT.

The only con is that the white color can be slightly see-through. We suggest opting for a darker shade or wearing a flesh-toned bra underneath. The color options are also a little limited, but we love the slate gray and light blue options.

WH editors have tried a lot of workout tops over the years and agree that the Lululemon Ebb to Street Tank is a reliable favorite. It’s super soft, seamless, sweat-wicking, and ultra stretchy thanks to a comfortable blend of elastane and nylon. Plus, it offers just the right amount of support and coverage for low-impact workouts like Pilates, yoga, and walking.

The most convenient part: it has a built-in shelf bra, so you don’t have to wear a separate bra underneath (unless you want to for more support!). And, we found that it actually does keep us feeling covered and protected during low-impact exercise, which we didn’t find to be the case with other tanks that have built-in bras. Another pro? The longer length of this top (it hits at the hip) keeps us feeling locked in and covered during workouts where we don’t want a ton of stomach showing.

“I love how versatile this top is, along with its longer length and the buttery-soft blend of nylon and elastane,” says trainer Kristina Earnest, CPT, founder of Kristina Earnest On Demand. This is her go-to top when she's working out at home, and she wears it under a sweater for her cold weather errands. It feels incredibly comfortable thanks to its seamless racerback design—no pesky straps, seams, or tightness to get in the way during your sweat session. However, you should note that because there are no adjustable straps, you can't customize the fit of this tank.We put this tank to the test during a variety of workouts, like HIIT training and running, and found that it’s not the best choice for high-intensity training. We noticed that it didn’t do a great job of holding the breasts in place during high-impact moves and felt the need for more padding and compression. But based on our extensive testing, it's difficult to find a tank with high-impact support. That's why we recommend pairing this with our favorite high-impact sports bra, the Under Armour Infinity 2.0 High Sports Bra.

This tank is meant to be worn over your favorite sports bra as an added layer of coverage. The loose, cropped fit is easy to throw on, and the round neck with the knotted side detail adds a touch of style. Our fave part about this tank? The cotton material is super breathable, so you never feel overheated or constricted like you would with a tighter top.

“I love that this top isn’t too tight,” says Jacqueline Kasen, CPT, trainer and senior director of group fitness at Anatomy. “It’s super comfortable and loose whether you’re running or lifting, but it still stays in place without bunching up.” Plus, it holds its shape after multiple washes and never pills or frays, making it as durable as it is functional.

The tank is made with cotton, but it also has a bit of spandex woven throughout, which makes it stretchy and not too sturdy. This is essential during workouts, as you want your top to move with you instead of against you at the gym.

We noticed that the shirt can look a bit boxy if you go too oversized, so we recommend sizing down for a more flattering, snug fit.

This short-sleeve workout top from Under Armour is a game changer for high-intensity exercise since it offers both compression and cooling technology. It’s designed as a baselayer to keep you protected under your sports gear (by preventing pinching and chafing), but we love to wear it on its own as a temperature-regulating, sweat-wicking, ultra-lightweight workout top.

This t-shirt has an ultra-tight, compressive fit (which can help with circulation and muscular endurance), but it is super thin and made with the brand's HeatGear fabric to keep you from overheating. It’s made from polyester for a smooth, lightweight feel, plus elastane for a generous amount of stretch. We love that this top is breathable and moisture-wicking, so even though it’s on the tighter side, it doesn’t feel like you’re wearing anything. Whether you’re lifting heavy or doing sprints outdoors, this top is perfect for keeping you cool, dry, and comfortable.

However, this top (like most compressive gear) might not be the best option for lower-impact exercise or workouts that require lots of stretching and bending—like yoga or Pilates. We generally opt for looser, softer fits for these workouts and stick to tighter tops for strength training and cardio.

Listen, I love a cute lil workout set as much as the next Pilates princess, but sometimes I don’t want to bare my entire midriff. That’s where an oversized workout top like this one comes in clutch. I especially love it in white because it's just a touch see-through, so it lets me show off my matching sports bra and workout short combo without feeling exposed. It’s also perfect for those days when I feel a little bloated and just need something loose-fitting that won’t emphasize my stomach.

Unlike the other workout tops you’ll find here, this tee is made from 100% cotton. The natural fiber is not exactly known for its high-performance abilities at the gym (it doesn’t wick sweat very well), but the thinner construction and super boxy fit help keep things super airy. I wore it for an outdoor workout over the summer and felt cool and comfortable—well, as much as you can while doing burpees.

It’s also just a really cute shape. I love the sleeves, which, despite being those of a t-shirt, hit at about the elbow, giving it a really laidback feel. I love it for working out or for throwing on over my skin-tight sets to and from Pilates, but I also find myself grabbing it day-to-day for errands and casual outings. I frequently throw it on over a bright tank styled with some longline biker shorts and comfy sneaks for a cool-girl fit that basically feels like I’m wearing pajamas.

As with any oversized workout tee, it might not be the wisest choice for HIIT workouts or anything super tactical. The billowing sleeves and loose fit could get caught on equipment or flop over your face while bending or jumping.

An open-back top is not only a super stylish choice, but it helps keep you cool as a cucumber during sweaty workouts too. I especially love this top for hot Pilates when I want as little fabric against my skin as possible. And since it has built-in padding (that doesn’t shift around in the wash!), I can literally just wear this on its own to yoga, Pilates, strength training, or other low-impact workouts.

I have worn this during my more intense exercise like an Orangetheory class or while running, and it does offer enough support for me (as someone who teeters between a C cup and a D cup). But if you have a larger chest size, it might be best to reserve this top for low-impact movement or layer it over your favorite sports bra.

Nike’s Dri-FIT tech (a blend of nylon and spandex) is also a major pro. It feels sleek and smooth against the skin but also helps move sweat away from the body. When I leave an intense workout class (even a heated one), it’s noticeable how much less sweat I have sitting between my skin and my workout top when I reach for this one, which is huge for me, since I’ve been struggling with eczema that gets worse with heat and sweat.

The material is also suuuper stretchy and comfortable, and never gets in the way of any kind of movement, whether that’s mat Pilates or an intense upper-body session at the gym. It’s also really lightweight so it feels nearly like a second skin and never requires adjustments mid-workout.

This tank from Gymshark pairs perfectly with the brand’s bestselling leggings. Like the leggings, this tank is nearly seamless—the only seams are along the bottom hem, but they are super flat and don’t cause any rubbing or chafing when up against the skin.

I love that the stretchy, smooth fabric (made of 96% nylon and 4% elastane) is basically sweat-proof and dries super quickly. That’s because nylon is a synthetic fabric that’s hydrophobic (i.e. it resists the penetration of water), so this top is great for wicking moisture away from the body during intense workouts.

Not only that, but it’s super cute, too. I love the square neckline and the length that hits right at the hip bones. It also has a built-in shelf bra to help support and lift the girls for a more flattering shape and a little extra protection against bounce during your workouts. It might not be enough support for larger chest sizes during high-intensity movement, but for smaller chests and moderate activity, it does the trick.

We’ve all been in those super stuffy gyms. When I start overheating during a workout, all I want is to be in a sports bra. But I’m not always comfortable showing my stomach. That’s where the Wild Thing Bra comes in. With its longline fit, which ends just above my belly button for slightly more coverage than a standard sports bra, the top leaves me feeling cool and comfortable at the gym.

And, of course, it’s super cute, thanks to the ruching in the center of the bra. I find this detail really flattering—it gives my stomach a snatched look when I wear it. While it doesn’t offer a ton of support since it’s on the lighter side, it provides decent compression for my higher intensity workouts. Its lightweight design and stylish details make it perfect for pairing with leggings at the gym, but I’ve also seen it styled with trousers or jeans for a sporty yet upscale vibe.

Worried about the lighter colors looking sheer? Don’t be! All styles come with removable cups to cover your girls, which is also helpful because you can take them out when you wash the bra. Speaking of washing, we find that this bra holds up well after multiple washes and never pills or changes in elasticity and shape.

One thing to keep in mind is that the straps aren’t adjustable, which can be a bit frustrating since it means you can’t customize the fit. I also wish it came in more colors.

This tank won the top spot in our Best Workout Tank Tops story for a few reasons: It's super flattering, never rides up, is compressive, supportive, and sweat-wicking.

We tested this ribbed tank across many different workouts—running, yoga, lifting, and HIIT—and we found that it never moves or rides up. And, we love that it’s a tighter spandex tank with high compression, as it holds us in all the right places and doesn’t allow for any boob bounce. It doesn’t have a built-in bra, but it’s tight enough to hold the chest in place.

It's also super moisture-wicking. WH assistant fashion editor Rose Lauture wore this during an intense workout and said there were no sweat stains, no matter which color she wore, thanks to the soft nylon. Because it's moisture-wicking, it also does a great job at preventing body odor.

This option offers some pretty solid support for not having a built-in bra (it's all about the compression!). However, it is made for medium support, so we like to wear an extra bra underneath this tank from time to time when our workouts get extra intense.

Stewart’s pro tip? For high-impact or high-intensity workouts, look for materials that are shiny and smooth to reduce chafing and wick sweat. For lower-intensity workouts, look for matte fabrics that have a soft, buttery texture for comfort and flexibility.

Should your workout top be loose or tight? While it does come down to personal preference, there are pros and cons to each. Tighter tops don’t get in the way of movement as much as a looser top might: they often feel more secure and supportive. But if you find looser tops more comfortable, they can certainly work for nearly every workout except for those involving a Pilates reformer (where loose fabric can easily get caught) or other equipment like cable machines.

Some workout tops have built-in shelf bras or bra pad inserts, but this can be a “love it or hate it” situation, says Stewart. For bigger busts or high-intensity activities, a built-in bra may not provide enough support, so you might need an extra sports bra underneath. However, for smaller busts, we find that they provide ample support for light- and medium-intensity workouts. “A built-in bra should feel supportive and not bothersome and shouldn’t be too tight or digging in to restrict your movement,” says Stewart.

There are tons of workout tops out there, so even finding the absolute best options to test was difficult. When I began the November 2026 update of this article, I revisited our original February 2024 story to see which of the picks our team is still wearing. Some of them are still team favorites, but I went back to the drawing board and asked fitness experts and stylists, including style contributors Kristen Saladino, Rose Lauture, and Lily Wohlner, which workout tops they were loving. From there, I removed the picks that we are no longer wearing and added the options we’ve been reaching for the most.

I made sure that the final picks on this list were options that we wear across all sorts of activities, from compressive options perfect for HIIT workout classes to lightweight, flowy tops we like for lounging or lower-impact exercise. Some of the contenders on this list come in tons of different colors and styles, have built-in bras, and are made with the highest-quality materials for their price points.

#6863. 6 Best Scalp Moisturizers, Tested by Editors and Dermatologists

Our top overall pick to fix a dry, itchy scalp is a budget-friendly serum from The Ordinary.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

A dry, itchy scalp can seriously mess with your day-to-day life. Beyond the maddening urge to scratch, there's the embarrassing component: dandruff and big white flakes studding your strands. And while dermatologists say that a dry, flaking scalp may be a sign of a medical condition called seborrheic dermatitis (this requires a prescription treatment like Zoryve), the best scalp moisturizers—hydrating shampoos for dry scalp, conditioners, and serums—can help.

"I always recommend starting with a gentle, sulfate-free shampoo that cleanses without stripping the scalp of its natural oils and following that up with a hydrating conditioner," says Ross Kopelman, MD, a hair transplant surgeon at Kopelman Hair Restoration in New York City. You can then follow that up with a leave-in treatment or a scalp oil to really lock in moisture.

It's also a good idea to limit hair washing to two to three times a week. "I suggest washing your hair with lukewarm water instead of hot and avoiding excessive heat styling, which can make things worse," Kopelman says. Before adding a few scalp moisturizers to your routine, here's what you should consider before shopping.

Below, shop the six best scalp moisturizers for all hair types, tested by editors and beauty experts.

The Women's Health team is regularly testing scalp moisturizers, and we found it's hard to beat this scalp serum from The Ordinary because of the exceptional value it offers. Yes, it's well-priced, but both dermatologists and testers alike love it. "It's great because it uses hyaluronic acid to deeply hydrate without being greasy," Dr. Kopelman says. He also notes that it's suitable for all hair types, including fine, medium, and thick hair, whether straight, wavy, curly, or coily.

Since I find myself constantly scratching my scalp throughout the day, I added this scalp serum to my hair routine, and after a few days of using it, I found that my symptoms significantly improved. Plus, as an added bonus, my thick, wavy hair appeared shinier and felt softer.

Our testers also like that this The Ordinary product is a leave-in treatment, too, since that makes application quick and easy (meaning there's no need to only use it on wash day).

This drugstore pick is another very well-priced scalp moisturizer that dermatologists say really works. "It has aloe, which soothes the skin, green tea extract, which contains antioxidants, and peptides, which help make the scalp skin healthy," says Morgan Rabach, MD, a dermatologist in New York City. It also has plenty of benefits for your hair: It enhances shine, reduces frizz, adds body, and helps prevent split ends.

Like our top overall pick, this is a convenient leave-in treatment. Users like the tingly sensation from the eucalyptus leaf extract, too, telling WH it not only feels refreshing but also smells great.

Our editors can't get enough of Briogeo's hair products—from the shampoo to hair masks, we love just about everything the brand puts out. This scalp moisturizer is no exception. It's powered by antioxidant-rich rosemary oil that supports scalp circulation and healthier hair follicles, castor oil for hydration, and tea tree to fight flakes and reduce itchiness. It's also formulated with bisabolol to calm irritation from an itchy scalp.

We love that you can tailor the treatment to suit your preferences—use it as a pre-shampoo rinse (a wait time of 10 to 15 minutes) or use it as an overnight hair mask. Either way, testers found the pointed tip on the bottle very easy to use, saying it made for a much more precise application.

Living Proof makes another fantastic leave-in treatment for dry scalps. Like our top overall pick, it's hyaluronic acid-based to offer lightweight (read: won't leave hair greasy) but intense hydration. It also contains vitamin B3 to help improve overall scalp health.

The product is easy to use, thanks to a precise applicator tip. And there's no rinsing required, so you can more seamlessly integrate it into your routine. Users say it works quickly, minimizing symptoms in just a few uses and almost completely eradicating any itchiness and flakes in just about two weeks. They love the smell, too—some say it's reminiscent of the ocean.

Dr. Rabach is also a fan of Moroccanoil's Dry Scalp Treatment, namely because it contains argan oil. "It contains both vitamin E and antioxidants," she says. It also contains salicylic acid, which is a gentle exfoliator that helps lift any flakes and can prevent scalp buildup that could lead to itchiness and more flakes in the future. Geranium oil also helps soothe the scalp and addresses any lingering dryness.

Like other Moroccanoil products, users overwhelmingly rave about this scalp treatment, saying it's hydrating, soothing, and smells great. We also love that a little bit goes a long way, which helps add to the overall value of the product.

If you have fine hair and want to avoid greasy-looking strands, reach for OUAI's scalp serum. "It's lightweight and doesn't make your hair greasy because it has glycerin and hyaluronic acid for moisture delivery," says Dr. Mian. It also has ingredients like red clover flower extract to help soothe an inflamed scalp while it heals. There are hair benefits, too, thanks to peptides that promote fuller, thicker strands.

It is the most costly serum on our list, but users say it's a worthy splurge for those who want something lightweight and effective with the added benefit of a great scent.

When shopping, look for ingredients like glycerin, coconut oil, jojoba oil, or argan oil, says Kiran Mian, MD, a dermatologist at Hudson Dermatology and Laser Surgery. When you have a dry scalp, it's also very important to avoid sulfates. "Sulfates, while great at creating a lather and deep clean, can be drying for the hair and scalp," Dr. Mian says.

Some scalp moisturizers are leave-in products, while others require a wait time before rinsing. Leave-in products tend to be more convenient, since you massage in and then can get on with your routine. However, those with very fine hair may find some leave-in products too heavy. If you're concerned about a scalp serum making your hair look greasy, you may want to consider a rinse-out product, which typically has a wait time of anywhere from 3 to 10 minutes. If you do choose to use a rinse-out product, though, make sure to allot time for washing your hair that day.

To find the best scalp moisturizers to quell itchiness and banish dandruff and white flakes, the Women's Health team consulted two dermatologists and a hair transplant surgeon, plus asked our team of anonymous testers and in-house editors for feedback on the products they use and love. We then put a handful of the top-rated products to the test, evaluating them for attributes like ingredient list, ease of use, and overall efficacy.
